| ci-hotfix | 2026-05-24 | **CI workflow `DIRECT_URL` env block hotfix — Sub-pattern 11 fire #2 (CI-parity).** v3.8.ajg added `directUrl = env("DIRECT_URL")` to `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` and updated both Render dashboard env vars + local `.env`. The third location — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` env blocks — was missed. Backend CI job failed at step `npx prisma validate` (line 30) with `P1012: Environment variable not found: DIRECT_URL`, 24-second failure on the v3.8.ajh push. E2E job skipped (needs: [backend, frontend]). Frontend job unaffected (doesn't touch Prisma). **Reproduced locally:** `unset DIRECT_URL && DATABASE_URL=...localhost... npx prisma validate` → exact same P1012. **Fix:** added `DIRECT_URL: postgresql://ci:ci@localhost:5432/ci` to both the backend job env block (line ~17) and the e2e job env block (line ~82). In CI both URLs point at the same plain Postgres container — no pooler involved so the split semantics don't matter; just need the env var present so `prisma validate` succeeds. **Verification:** `DATABASE_URL=... DIRECT_URL=... npx prisma validate` → "The schema at prisma/schema.prisma is valid 🚀". **Methodology principle (canonical extension banked):** Sub-pattern 11 (CI-parity-verification) — when adding any `env()` reference to `schema.prisma`, the env block must be updated in FOUR locations: (1) local `backend/.env`, (2) Render dashboard env vars, (3) CI workflow env blocks for every job that runs Prisma, (4) CLAUDE.md §2.2 canonical env var list. ajg updated 1, 2, 4; missed 3. Second fire of Sub-pattern 11 in the session arc (first was Sprint 51 `013883d` where local tsc passed but next-lint ESLint failed at CI). Banking the four-location checklist as Sub-pattern 11 extension; pattern fire count for next quarterly §19 review goes from 19 → 20. **Docs-only per §3.1** (CI workflow change doesn't affect deployed user-visible artifacts) — no version bump. **Pre-push verification gate:** local `prisma validate` with CI-shape env vars passed before pushing. Same gate Sub-pattern 11 prescribed but I missed running on ajg. |

| v3.8.ajh | 2026-05-24 | **Sprint 4 of carrier onboarding lifecycle epic — INFO_REQUESTED workflow (AE create + carrier resolve + auto-flip status + dual email).** Originally banked as Sprint 1's deferred half; closes the gap that the ajd 6-state machine introduced (carriers could land in INFO_REQUESTED status from cron flips but had no UI surface to actually resolve anything). **Schema (manual migration per §2.2):** 1 new model + 2 enums. `InfoRequest` model with `carrierId` (cascade delete with carrier), `createdById` (AE who created — no-action delete preserves audit), `cancelledById` (optional, AE who cancelled), `category` (InfoRequestCategory enum), `message` (free-form TEXT), `status` (OPEN/RESOLVED/CANCELLED), `resolvedNote` + `resolvedAt` (carrier response), `cancelledAt`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. Indexed on `(carrierId, status)` for the carrier-portal open-requests query + `createdAt` for AE chronological list. `InfoRequestCategory` enum — 9 values matching industry-standard onboarding asks: `COI_UPDATE`, `W9_UPDATE`, `AUTHORITY_LETTER`, `SAFETY_CLARIFICATION`, `EIN_VERIFICATION`, `VOIDED_CHECK`, `ADDRESS_PROOF`, `REFERENCES`, `OTHER`. `InfoRequestStatus` enum — 3 values: OPEN/RESOLVED/CANCELLED. User model gets two new relations: `createdInfoRequests` + `cancelledInfoRequests`; CarrierProfile gets `infoRequests`. **Service (`infoRequestService.ts`):** encapsulates create/resolve/cancel with status auto-flip side effects, all wrapped in `prisma.$transaction` so request mutation + carrier status update succeed atomically. Industry-standard template strings shipped inline as `CATEGORY_TEMPLATES` — **deleted in v3.8.bao**, which found the export unconsumed and moved the templates to the AE modal that actually renders them. The pre-fill behaviour is unchanged. Email helpers `sendInfoRequestEmail` (carrier-facing) + `sendInfoRequestResolvedEmail` (AE-facing) — Resend transactional with §2.1 brand-canonical chrome (cream FBF7F0 panel, gold-dark BA7517 CTA, navy 0A2540 headings, Playfair Display H2). Carrier email reply-to = compliance@silkroutelogistics.ai (Lead Hunter outreach gets reply-to = whaider@ per §3.10 but operational compliance asks belong in the compliance inbox). **Auto-flip logic (canonical state machine extension):** AE creates first OPEN request → carrier `onboardingStatus` flips PENDING/REVIEWING → INFO_REQUESTED. **Superseded in v3.8.ban:** the terminal states are no longer "defensively skipped" — `createInfoRequest` now REFUSES them with 409 `CARRIER_NOT_UNDER_REVIEW` before the transaction opens, because skipping the flip while still writing the row and sending the email was not a gate. Per §13.3 Item 260. Carrier resolves last OPEN request → INFO_REQUESTED → REVIEWING. AE cancels last OPEN request → INFO_REQUESTED → REVIEWING. "Last OPEN" detected via `tx.infoRequest.count({ where: { carrierId, status: "OPEN" } }) === 0` inside the same transaction. **Backend endpoints (3 AE + 2 carrier):** `POST /api/info-requests` (AE create), `GET /api/info-requests?carrierId=X` (AE list — the `&status=Y` filter this row originally described was deleted in v3.8.bao, zero callers), `PATCH /api/info-requests/:id/cancel` (AE cancel) — all under ADMIN/CEO authz mirroring the existing approve/reject buttons. `GET /api/carrier-auth/info-requests` returns OPEN requests for the authenticated carrier (resolved/cancelled omitted from carrier view — only what they need to act on). `POST /api/carrier-auth/info-requests/:id/resolve` validates carrier owns the request + min 1 char response, then delegates to `resolveInfoRequest` service. **AE Console UI:** new `components/carriers/InfoRequestModal.tsx` with category dropdown + message textarea + auto-fill template logic (preserves manual edits across category picks so AE can write custom then verify category without losing text). New "Request Info" button on `/dashboard/carriers` action bar (amber `bg-amber-500/20` to match `INFO_REQUESTED` status pill), gated to ADMIN/CEO + non-APPROVED/REJECTED/SUSPENDED. Modal mounted at root level so the fixed-position overlay doesn't clip against the side panel's `overflow:auto`. **Carrier portal UI:** `application-status/page.tsx` `InfoRequestedSection` rewritten from defensive placeholder copy to real implementation. Fetches via TanStack `useQuery` against `/carrier-auth/info-requests`, renders each OPEN request as an `InfoRequestCard` with category eyebrow + message + per-request resolve textarea + Send Response button. On submit, the mutation invalidates BOTH `["carrier-info-requests"]` AND `["carrier-application-status"]` so the section re-renders into the REVIEWING state automatically (no page reload) when the last open request is resolved. Empty-state success message ("All caught up — your application is back in active review") shows when `requests.length === 0` (defensive — auto-flip should have moved status to REVIEWING by then, but the UI handles the transitional moment cleanly). **Scope:** ~750 LOC net across 9 files (schema + migration + infoRequestService + routes/infoRequests + carrierAuth.ts +carrier endpoints + routes/index.ts mount + carriers/page.tsx button + InfoRequestModal component + application-status InfoRequestedSection rewrite + VersionFooter) + 1 Prisma migration. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `npx prisma generate` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx next build` clean (`/carrier/dashboard/application-status` 8.28 kB → 8.89 kB; AE `/dashboard/carriers` route bundle absorbed +1.5kB for modal component). **P1002 risk:** ZERO — the v3.8.ajg DIRECT_URL split is now in place. The new migration `20260524123000_add_info_request` will route through the direct connection and apply cleanly. **Banked for v3.8.aji (next sprint):** file-upload attachments as part of carrier resolution. Sprint shape: extend `InfoRequest` with `Document[]` relation (or add `attachmentDocumentIds: String[]`), reuse existing `multer.array` + `storageService.uploadFile` infra from `routes/carriers.ts:335`, extend the carrier-portal resolve form with file input + drag-drop, surface attachment links in the AE list endpoint + the resolved-email body. ~120 LOC + 1 migration. **Banked for further extension:** AE drawer that surfaces the full info-request thread per carrier (read + write surface beyond the bulk modal); INFO_REQUESTED template editor for AE custom templates per carrier-type; resolved-request audit visibility on the carrier-portal side (post-RESOLVED carriers don't see historical resolved threads in v3.8.ajh). **Patterns applied:** §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship (backend + AE UI + carrier UI in one commit since the workflow is one feature); §3.5 audit-first (confirmed NotificationType is String-typed not enum so any new "INFO_REQUEST" string is valid; verified sendEmail/wrap signature; confirmed existing approve/reject button placement for visual symmetry); §2.2 manual Prisma migration; §A.1 state machine canonical extension (status auto-flip encoded in service layer, not scattered across controllers); §19 Pattern 7 design-system conformance (modal chrome + carrier card use brand-canonical tokens — cream FBF7F0 surface, gold-dark BA7517 CTA, warning amber FBEFD4/B07A1A for INFO_REQUESTED state, danger F6E3E3/9B2C2C for errors); §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity (all gates clean). **Patterns emerged:** none. **Letter:** ajg latest origin/main HEAD; ajh sequence-continuous on top. |

| v3.8.ajg | 2026-05-24 | **Prisma + Neon `DIRECT_URL` split — permanent fix for the P1002 advisory-lock contention class (closes §13.3 Item 191).** Ships the canonical Prisma + Neon dual-URL pattern documented by both vendors. **Schema change:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` `datasource db { ... }` block adds `directUrl = env("DIRECT_URL")` alongside the existing `url = env("DATABASE_URL")` line. Prisma now auto-uses `DIRECT_URL` for ALL migrate operations (`migrate deploy`, `migrate dev`, `migrate status`, `migrate resolve`, `db push`) while runtime queries continue to use `DATABASE_URL` (the pooled connection). **Env vars added on Render dashboard (pre-deploy by Wasi):** `DIRECT_URL` = Neon's direct endpoint connection string (no `-pooler` in hostname; obtained from Neon dashboard → Connect → "Pooled connection" toggle OFF → copy resulting string). `DATABASE_URL` unchanged (stays pooled). `PRISMA_MIGRATE_LOCK_TIMEOUT=60000` recommended as belt-and-suspenders (bumps lock-acquisition timeout from 10s default to 60s). **Why this is the permanent fix:** the advisory lock `pg_advisory_lock(72707369)` is session-scoped — released only when the connection holding it actually closes. Neon's pooled endpoint keeps connections alive between requests and rotates PIDs across the pool, so a migrate process that exits normally still leaves its lock-holder PID cached in the pooler. Lock doesn't release until Neon's idle timer expires that specific cached connection (~5min on Launch tier). The direct endpoint has no pooler caching layer — the connection closes the moment migrate exits, the lock releases instantly, and the next migrate (whenever it fires) acquires the lock cleanly with no contention. Three prior fires (v3.8.ajf push being the most recent) all self-healed via the wait-and-retry pattern; this commit eliminates the class. **CLAUDE.md §2.2 updated** with canonical env var list including `DATABASE_URL` + `DIRECT_URL` + `PRISMA_MIGRATE_LOCK_TIMEOUT` + the local-dev requirement that `backend/.env` mirror the same pair. **Side benefits beyond P1002 elimination:** `prisma migrate status` becomes deterministic (currently it also runs through the pooler and can give stale answers about applied-vs-pending migrations); the Sprint 44b `prisma migrate status` post-deploy verification gate becomes reliable instead of probabilistic; future destructive migrations (enum-swaps like v3.8.ajd, column-drops, table-renames) become safer because the lock is held by a connection that genuinely owns the migration without pooler interference. **No migration in this sprint** — schema change is metadata-only (adding `directUrl` to datasource block changes how Prisma routes connections, not what tables exist). No data write. No `migrate deploy` blocking step beyond the existing Sprint 44b canonical buildCommand. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `npx prisma generate` clean (regenerates client with new datasource shape), backend `tsc --noEmit` clean (no code references the datasource directly so no type cascade). **Post-deploy verification:** next migration-bearing deploy should complete in <5s with no P1002 even if a prior deploy was recent. Render build log should still show `prisma migrate deploy` running; the difference is the connection underneath is now direct, not pooled. **Risks considered + rejected (re-stated from Item 191 for permanent record):** `PRISMA_SCHEMA_DISABLE_ADVISORY_LOCK=1` (allows concurrent destructive migrations to corrupt — rejected); pre-deploy `pg_advisory_unlock(72707369)` SQL hook (race condition with legit concurrent migrations — rejected); switching off Neon to another managed Postgres (over-engineering — rejected). **Methodology observation:** Sprint 44b's baseline-reset + canonical buildCommand work hardened the migration history + verification gate but did NOT address the connection-URL split. The class of bug was latent through the entire Sprint 44 → ajf arc; three independent fires in <2 weeks validated the recurrence rate as material enough to justify this architectural fix vs continuing to absorb the manual-retry cost. **Scope:** ~22 LOC net (5 LOC schema datasource block + 9 LOC §2.2 env vars list + 8 LOC §11 row) + 2 env vars on Render dashboard + 1 local `.env` line (Wasi-managed, not in repo). **Patterns applied:** §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship (schema + docs in one commit since the schema change is the load-bearing artifact); §3.5 audit-first (three-fire-validated P1002 pattern banked in §13.3 Item 191 BEFORE shipping the fix); §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity. **Patterns emerged:** none. **Letter:** docs-only P1002 banking commit (`c7db912`) was unversioned per §3.1; ajf was the latest versioned HEAD; ajg sequence-continuous on top. |

| docs-only | 2026-05-24 | **P1002 advisory-lock incident #3 of session arc + permanent-fix sprint plan banked (no code/migration change; docs-only meta per §3.1).** Third P1002 fire in the v3.8.a* range: v3.8.ail (initial occurrence), v3.8.aix (second), and v3.8.ajf push (this incident — Render build log showed `Timed out trying to acquire a postgres advisory lock (SELECT pg_advisory_lock(72707369)). Timeout: 10000ms` after `npx prisma migrate deploy` resolved 9 migrations and got blocked on the hardcoded Prisma lock 72707369). **Pattern characterization (now three-fire-validated):** Prisma's `migrate deploy` acquires a Postgres advisory lock (hardcoded number `72707369` in Prisma source) before applying migrations. Lock is session-scoped — released only when the connection holding it actually closes. Today's `DATABASE_URL` points at Neon's POOLED endpoint (`-pooler` hostname), which intentionally keeps connections alive between requests and rotates PIDs across the pool. Net effect: migrate process exits normally, but its lock-holder connection stays cached in Neon's pooler. Lock doesn't release until Neon's idle timer expires that specific cached connection (~5 min on Launch tier). Every `migrate deploy` thereafter plays roulette against whichever pooler-cached PID is currently holding the lock. P1002 frequency is roughly correlated with migration-bearing deploy frequency. **Resolution pattern (proven on three fires):** wait 5-30 min for Neon idle timeout → Manual Deploy in Render dashboard → migrate deploy acquires lock cleanly, applies pending migrations, deploy succeeds. Self-healing 100% of the time across the three fires; no manual unlock SQL ever needed. The v3.8.aix → aiy retry mechanic (next push auto-retries even if frontend-only) banked as Item 71's "P1002-advisory-lock-retry-via-next-push" methodology observation. **Permanent fix sprint (logged as §13.3 Item 191 — "Prisma + Neon DIRECT_URL split"):** the canonical Prisma + Neon pattern documented by both vendors. Split runtime + migrate connection URLs: `DATABASE_URL` stays on the pooled endpoint (runtime benefits from connection limits); new `DIRECT_URL` env var points at Neon's direct endpoint (no pooler in hostname). Schema's `datasource db` block adds `directUrl = env("DIRECT_URL")`; Prisma automatically uses `directUrl` for migrate operations when present. Direct connection releases the advisory lock the moment migrate exits — no pooler caching layer between Prisma and Postgres. ~10 LOC schema change + 2 env var additions (Render dashboard + local `.env`) + 1 CLAUDE.md §2.2 update. **Belt-and-suspenders secondary hardening logged in same sprint:** `PRISMA_MIGRATE_LOCK_TIMEOUT=60000` env var on Render bumps Prisma's lock-acquisition timeout from 10s to 60s, reduces residual P1002 frequency on the direct URL itself. **Risks considered + rejected for the permanent fix:** (a) `PRISMA_SCHEMA_DISABLE_ADVISORY_LOCK=1` — disables the lock entirely; would allow two concurrent migrate processes to apply destructive migrations simultaneously (enum-swap class would corrupt); not recommended. (b) Pre-deploy SQL hook running `pg_advisory_unlock(72707369)` — race condition with legitimate concurrent migrations; not recommended. (c) Switching off Neon to another managed Postgres — over-engineering for this specific class. **Side benefits of the DIRECT_URL split:** `prisma migrate status` becomes deterministic (currently also runs through the pooler and can give stale answers); the Sprint 44b `prisma migrate status --exit-code` post-gate becomes reliable instead of probabilistic; future destructive migrations safer. **Scope:** ~10 LOC + env updates + ~30-60 min execution time (mostly Neon dashboard navigation + Render env editing). **Priority:** elevated for after the v3.8.ajf P1002 self-heals + the three pending migrations apply — every future schema migration is at risk of P1002 until this lands. Pairs naturally with §13.3 Item 8.10's deploy-chain hardening lineage (Sprint 44a → 44b → 44c → 44d). |

| v3.8.ajf | 2026-05-24 | **Sprint 3 of carrier onboarding lifecycle epic — unusual-activity dual-channel OTP (email + SMS via OpenPhone).** Layers on aje's geolocation foundation. Direct continuation of the user directive: *"if any unusual activity is detected an OTP request gets sent on the mobile phone number or email."* **Schema (manual migration per §2.2):** two nullable TEXT columns on `users` — `lastLoginIp` + `lastLoginCountry`. Written inside the OTP-verify (and TOTP-verify) success paths AFTER JWT issuance, so each row reflects "last successfully completed login" rather than "last attempt." First-login behavior intentionally permissive — pre-ajf users have null `lastLoginCountry` so their first post-deploy login is NOT flagged as unusual; baseline starts from that point forward. **Detection (`geoService.detectUnusualActivity`):** new exported helper. Compares current attempt's `resolveCountry(currentIp)` against `user.lastLoginCountry`. Returns `{ isUnusual, reason, currentCountry, lastKnownCountry }`. Two guards: (a) no `lastLoginCountry` recorded → not unusual (first-login baseline build); (b) current country unresolvable (private-network IP, localhost, geoip-lite miss) → not unusual (defensive — local dev would otherwise fire every login). Different country than baseline → unusual with human-readable `reason` ("Login from KR, last known from US"). **Deferred detection lenses noted in service comment:** same-country city-jump (1500+mi US — needs Haversine + lat/lon), VPN/datacenter exit nodes (needs paid IP-reputation API), time-of-day anomalies (needs per-user login histogram). All deferred until BKN onboarding produces a case. **OpenPhone SMS wrapper:** new `sendOtpSms(phone, code)` in `openPhoneService.ts`. Wraps existing `sendSMS()` (already wired at line 45 — confirmed pre-aje audit). Body intentionally minimal to fit single SMS segment (~160 chars): "Silk Route Logistics security code: {code}. We detected this login from a new location. Code expires in 5 minutes. If this wasn't you, reset your password." **Carrier login integration (`carrierAuth.ts`):** new imports for `detectUnusualActivity` + `sendOtpSms`. In `POST /login` step 1 (BEFORE OTP send): compute `unusualResult = detectUnusualActivity({ currentIp, lastLoginCountry: user.lastLoginCountry })`. Always send email OTP via existing `sendOtpEmail`. THEN, if `unusualResult.isUnusual && user.phone`, also fire `sendOtpSms` (non-fatal: email is primary channel, SMS enhancement) AND write a `SystemLog` WARNING with `source: "carrierAuth-unusual-activity"` for AE forensic review. Carrier-facing response unchanged from the existing `{ pendingOtp: true, email }` — fraudster doesn't learn we detected the unusual signal. **lastLogin* updates:** in BOTH `POST /verify-otp` and `POST /totp-verify` success paths (after JWT issuance), write `lastLoginIp` + `lastLoginCountry` + `lastLogin` (the latter existed but was set elsewhere via middleware; now also written here for consistency). Failure is logged but non-fatal — login still succeeds even if the geo-write fails. **No frontend changes** — same OTP flow from the carrier's perspective. They may simply also receive a text alongside the email when the platform classifies the attempt as unusual. **Threat model:** assumes account compromise typically captures password + email but not phone. SMS provides an out-of-band channel where the fraudster cannot intercept the code even with full email access. SMS-only delivery is NOT used (carrier without phone on file still completes login via email OTP); dual-channel is the strictly-more-secure variant. **Scope:** ~140 LOC net across 4 files (schema + migration + geoService extension + openPhoneService sendOtpSms + carrierAuth login/verify-otp/totp-verify) + 1 Prisma migration. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `npx prisma generate` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean (no FE changes — sanity check), `npx next build` clean. **P1002 risk:** medium — new Prisma migration. Retry via Manual Deploy if hit. **Banked for future expansion:** (a) city-jump Haversine detection (same country, distant city — banked when first need surfaces); (b) paid IP-reputation API for VPN/datacenter flags (Sprint C per CLAUDE.md original — deferred until BKN); (c) time-of-day histogram per user (longer-term ML signal); (d) carrier ability to register "trusted devices" so cross-country travel doesn't trigger SMS every login (worth doing after first BKN-class carrier hits the friction). **Patterns applied:** §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship; §3.5 audit-first (confirmed OpenPhone sendSMS exists, geoip-lite resolveCountry from aje is reusable, identified OTP-verify + TOTP-verify success paths as the two write sites for lastLogin*); §2.2 manual Prisma migration; §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity; defensive non-fatal SMS dispatch per email-primary channel design. **Patterns emerged:** none. Direct continuation of aje's geo foundation — registrationCountry + emailVerifiedFromCountry + lastLoginCountry now form a three-point baseline (signup + verify-click + login) for future fraud-detection layers. **Letter:** aje latest origin/main HEAD; ajf sequence-continuous on top. |

| v3.8.aje | 2026-05-24 | **Sprint 2 of carrier onboarding lifecycle epic — email verification gate + offline IP geolocation (atomic bundle).** Closes the canonical signup-fraud filter gap the ajc/ajd brainstorm + read-only assessment surfaced. Pre-aje a carrier could register with `dispatch@madeup-mc.com` and the platform had no proof they controlled that inbox until login-time OTP — but login-time OTP is opt-in (carrier chooses when to log in), so application submission completed without any inbox proof. **Schema (manual migration per §2.2):** four nullable columns split across two tables — `users.emailVerifiedAt` + `emailVerifiedFromIp` + `emailVerifiedFromCountry` (raw IP retained for AE forensic inspection; country resolved via geoip-lite offline lookup at click time); `carrier_profiles.registrationCountry` (computed from req.ip at registration time, stored plaintext for AE inspection — distinct from the existing chameleon `registrationIpHash` which is sha256'd). No backfill. The legacy `User.isVerified` Boolean field stays — it's currently overloaded to mean "AE-approved" (set at approval time, not at email-confirm time), and rewriting 27+ consumers to disambiguate is out of scope. `emailVerifiedAt` is the canonical "this carrier proved control of the inbox" timestamp going forward. **geoip-lite (zero-cost offline DB):** added to `backend/package.json` + `@types/geoip-lite` to dev. ~50MB binary database ships with the build; resolves IPv4 + IPv6 to country (>99% accuracy per MaxMind GeoLite2 source), region, city. No per-request cost. Does NOT give VPN/datacenter/ASN-reputation signals — that requires a paid API (Sprint C deferred per CLAUDE.md until BKN-onboarding produces a case). New `services/geoService.ts` exports `resolveGeo`, `resolveCountry`, `extractClientIp` — the last accounts for Render's `app.set("trust proxy", 1)` at server.ts:35 and handles IPv6-mapped IPv4 (`::ffff:` prefix) normalization. **Email verification token storage:** extends the existing `RESET:` prefix pattern at `otpService.ts:106` — verification tokens stored in the OtpCode table with a `VERIFY:` prefix. No new schema for token storage. 24-hour expiry (more lenient than 5-min login OTPs since carriers may not check email immediately post-signup). New helpers `createEmailVerificationToken` + `peekEmailVerificationToken` + `consumeEmailVerificationToken` + `getEmailVerificationResendCooldown` (60s cooldown). **Email send:** new `sendEmailVerificationEmail` in `emailService.ts` — Resend transactional email with §2.1 brand-canonical chrome (navy #0A2540, gold-dark #BA7517, cream #FBF7F0, warning amber #FBEFD4/#B07A1A), Playfair Display H2, prominent gold-dark CTA button, fallback plaintext URL block, expiry warning. **`registerCarrier` integration (`carrierController.ts`):** imports `extractClientIp` + `resolveCountry` + `createEmailVerificationToken` + `sendEmailVerificationEmail`. Captures `registrationIp` + `registrationCountry` BEFORE the prisma.user.create so the country is persisted on CarrierProfile in the same write. After response (fire-and-forget per the rest of the post-registration chain), fires `createEmailVerificationToken` → builds `https://silkroutelogistics.ai/carrier/verify-email?token=<64-char-hex>` URL → calls `sendEmailVerificationEmail`. **Compass auto-approve gate (`carrierController.ts:325-345`):** A-grade auto-approve now ALSO checks `emailVerifiedAt` via a fresh DB read (since Compass vetting runs async, the carrier may have verified between registration and Compass returning). Unverified carriers stay PENDING even when FMCSA + insurance + OFAC + Compass all pass — AE manual approval has its own discretion; the auto-path is held until inbox control is proven. **New endpoint `POST /api/carrier-auth/verify-email`:** PUBLIC (no auth middleware — token IS the auth). Peek-then-consume pattern from v3.7.m: peek validates without burning; transactional update marks `emailVerifiedAt` + captures `emailVerifiedFromIp` + resolves and writes `emailVerifiedFromCountry` AND consumes the token atomically — if either write fails, both roll back so the token remains usable for retry. Idempotent for already-verified users (returns `{ verified: true, alreadyVerified: true }`). Geo-mismatch detection: when `registrationCountry !== emailVerifiedFromCountry` AND both are non-null, writes a `SystemLog` WARNING row with `source: "emailVerification"` for AE forensic review. Carrier-facing response never surfaces the mismatch (avoids cluing in a fraudster about the detection). **New endpoint `POST /api/carrier-auth/resend-verification`:** carrier-authenticated, ADMIN/CEO not required, 60s cooldown via `getEmailVerificationResendCooldown` returning ms-until-next-allowed; 429 response includes the countdown in the error message. Already-verified callers get 409. **Application-status endpoint extended:** `User.emailVerifiedAt` added to the select + returned in response. Frontend banner key. **New frontend page `/carrier/verify-email`:** Suspense-wrapped (Next.js static export requires this for `useSearchParams()`). Reads `?token=...` from URL → POSTs to `/api/carrier-auth/verify-email` → renders five states (loading / success / alreadyVerified / invalid / error) with brand-canonical chrome. Login CTA links to `/carrier/login` for next step. Outside `/carrier/dashboard/*` tree because the carrier may not be logged in when they click the link. **Application-status page extension:** new top-of-card warning-amber banner renders above state-specific content when `emailVerifiedAt` is null. MailCheck icon + carrier email surfaced + Resend Verification button (TanStack mutation; 429 message surfaced verbatim; success path invalidates the status query so the banner disappears the moment the carrier clicks the link from their inbox). **AE drawer geo-mismatch surface:** banked for follow-up (current AE Carriers page doesn't have a per-carrier drawer; SystemLog row carries the signal until a drawer surface lands). **Scope:** ~480 LOC net across 10 files (schema + migration + geoService + emailService + otpService + carrierController + carrierAuth + verify-email page + application-status page + VersionFooter) + 1 Prisma migration + 2 npm dependencies. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `npx prisma generate` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean (TS7016 caught + fixed via @types/geoip-lite install), frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx next build` clean (`/carrier/verify-email` 3.52 kB + `/carrier/dashboard/application-status` 8.28 kB both prerendered static). **P1002 risk:** medium — new Prisma migration; if Render auto-deploy hits advisory-lock contention, retry via Manual Deploy after Neon idle-connection timeout. **Banked for v3.8.ajf (next sprint):** unusual-activity dual-channel OTP. User directive: "if any unusual activity is detected an OTP request gets sent on the mobile phone number or email." Sprint shape: track `User.lastLoginIp` + `User.lastLoginCountry` (new schema fields) + detection helper that compares current login geo to last-known geo via `resolveCountry` (already shipped here) + when current country differs from last-login country, send OTP via OpenPhone SMS (sendSMS already wired at `openPhoneService.ts:45`) in addition to email OTP. OpenPhone SMS infra confirmed pre-aje audit. **Patterns applied:** §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship (email-verify + geolocation bundled per user directive — verify-click is exactly where geolocation pays off); §3.5 audit-first (read-only assessment surfaced `User.isVerified` overload, OpenPhone SMS infra exists for ajf, OtpCode token reuse pattern, `registrationIpHash` already wired); §2.2 manual Prisma migration; §3.10 transactional email via Resend; §19 Pattern 7 design-system conformance (warning-amber palette FBEFD4/B07A1A on banner matches the page's INFO_REQUESTED status pill); §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity (all gates clean). **Patterns emerged:** none. **Letter:** ajd latest origin/main HEAD; aje sequence-continuous on top. |

| v3.8.ajd | 2026-05-24 | **Sprint 1 of carrier onboarding lifecycle epic — 6-state OnboardingStatus + non-APPROVED login refactor + carrier application-status page.** Foundation commit for the post-submit → approve workflow gap surfaced during the ajc brainstorm. State machine, login gate, and read-only carrier-portal status surface ship together; v3.8.aje will land the INFO_REQUESTED workflow on top of this. **Enum refactor (manual migration per §2.2):** OnboardingStatus condensed from legacy 6-value (PENDING / DOCUMENTS_SUBMITTED / UNDER_REVIEW / APPROVED / REJECTED / SUSPENDED) to canonical 6-value (PENDING / REVIEWING / INFO_REQUESTED / APPROVED / REJECTED / SUSPENDED). REVIEWING merges legacy DOCUMENTS_SUBMITTED + UNDER_REVIEW (the AE didn't operationally distinguish between them); INFO_REQUESTED added for v3.8.aje workflow. Migration `20260524093000_onboarding_status_6_states/migration.sql` uses canonical enum-swap pattern: drop PENDING defaults on both `carrier_profiles.onboardingStatus` + `customers.onboardingStatus` → cast each column with CASE remapping (DOCUMENTS_SUBMITTED + UNDER_REVIEW → REVIEWING; others pass through) → DROP old type → RENAME new → restore PENDING defaults. Customer side rides through unchanged (shipper onboarding never used the two retired values). **Carrier login refactor (`carrierAuth.ts`):** non-APPROVED carriers may NOW log in. The OTP + TOTP gates previously 403'd on any status != APPROVED with state-specific friendly messages; now they only hard-block SUSPENDED. PENDING / REVIEWING / INFO_REQUESTED / REJECTED all let the carrier through to JWT issuance. Why: those carriers need a portal surface to see their application status (Wasi directive: *"carriers... able to login to carrier portal and see the status of the application when it is reviewed or touched, if any new docs are needed"*). Per-route APPROVED checks at backend (`carrierLoads.ts:31/169`, etc.) defend against a stale-tab carrier hitting load endpoints; defense-in-depth holds. **New endpoint:** `GET /api/carrier-auth/application-status` returns header context (companyName / mcNumber / dotNumber), onboardingStatus, submittedAt, approvedAt. Shape designed stable so v3.8.aje can extend with `infoRequests[]` + `rejectionReason` + `reapplyEligibleAt` without contract churn. **Carrier dashboard layout (`carrier/dashboard/layout.tsx`):** status-based routing — non-APPROVED carriers redirected to `/carrier/dashboard/application-status`; APPROVED carriers redirected OFF the status page back to the main dashboard. Sidebar + search bar + notifications bell + Marco Polo AI all hidden for non-APPROVED carriers (the only accessible route is the status page; no navigation needed). Notifications query `enabled` flag gated to APPROVED — saves the 2-minute polling cycle. **New page `frontend/src/app/carrier/dashboard/application-status/page.tsx`:** state-specific render. Header card shows companyName + MC# + DOT# + status pill (brand-canonical status palette: PENDING/INFO_REQUESTED warning amber, REVIEWING info navy-100, APPROVED success, REJECTED/SUSPENDED danger). Body section renders per-state content — PENDING ("Your application is in the queue" + 3-step "what happens next" ladder), REVIEWING ("A reviewer is on your application" + reassurance copy), INFO_REQUESTED (defensive copy pointing carriers to email/compliance@; v3.8.aje wires the actual InfoRequest list + resolve form here), REJECTED (defensive copy; v3.8.aje wires reason + reapply date), APPROVED (defensive — carrier should be redirecting off this page anyway). Footer compliance contact card always rendered. 60s `refetchInterval` so status flips surface without page reload. **AE Console updates:** `STATUS_COLORS` palette on `/dashboard/carriers` extended to the new 6 states (REVIEWING navy-100, INFO_REQUESTED amber, SUSPENDED gray); tab-filter dropdown updated to enumerate the new 6 states; Zod validator on the AE update endpoint (`routes/carriers.ts:49`) updated to the new enum; cron filter on `cron/index.ts:349` (identity-validation for pending carriers) updated from `["PENDING","UNDER_REVIEW","DOCUMENTS_SUBMITTED"]` → `["PENDING","REVIEWING"]` (INFO_REQUESTED carriers are mid-conversation, identity already validated). One stale literal at `routes/carriers.ts:468` (`UNDER_REVIEW` → `REVIEWING`) caught by backend tsc enum check post-Prisma-generate. **Scope:** ~280 LOC net across 8 files (schema + migration + carrierAuth + cron + carriers.ts validator + AE carriers page + carrier dashboard layout + new application-status page) + 1 Prisma migration. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `npx prisma generate` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean (1 stale literal caught + fixed), frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx next build` clean (`/carrier/dashboard/application-status` 5.58 kB prerendered static). **Banked for v3.8.aje (next sprint):** `InfoRequest` Prisma model + AE create endpoint + carrier view/resolve endpoint + AE Console "Request Info" UI with industry-standard templates + carrier-facing resolve UI on the status page; `CarrierProfile.rejectionReason` + `rejectedAt` + `rejectedById` + `reapplyEligibleAt` schema fields + per-reason reapply windows + REJECTED state UI on status page. Per Wasi answers: 6 lifecycle states (this commit), full login from day one (this commit), skip OCR (deferred indefinitely while volume is low), industry-standard INFO_REQUESTED templates (v3.8.aje), per-reason reapply windows (v3.8.aje). **Patterns applied:** §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship (state machine + login + read-only status page in one commit; INFO_REQUESTED workflow held for v3.8.aje per Wasi "Start with sprint 1 first" directive); §3.5 audit-first (verified 27 backend + 16 frontend onboardingStatus consumers before migration design — narrow grep showed most consume APPROVED literal which stays unchanged); §2.2 manual Prisma migration (enum-swap pattern with CASE remapping); §A.1 state machine canonical (6-state lifecycle now codified); §19 Pattern 7 design-system conformance (STATUS_COLORS palette extended to new 6 states with brand-canonical color tokens); §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity (full pre-push gates clean). **Patterns emerged:** none — Sub-pattern 8 visual-verification gate not applicable to this sprint since no PENDING/REVIEWING fixture exists in current seed (Wasi will manually verify post-deploy on the first PENDING carrier hitting the page; banking visual-verification for the v3.8.aje session). **Letter:** ajc latest origin/main HEAD; ajd sequence-continuous on top. |

Closes the structural gap surfaced by the 2026-06-12 three-agent carrier-portal audit: the `Driver` model ([schema.prisma:2452](backend/prisma/schema.prisma#L2452)) had NO relation to `CarrierProfile` or `User` — pure AE-console fleet scaffolding (`/dashboard/drivers` + `/dashboard/fleet`) — and the carrier portal's existing driver assignment writes free-text `Load.driverName`/`driverPhone` only ([carrierLoads.ts:362-365](backend/src/routes/carrierLoads.ts#L362)). No carrier-owned roster could exist, so the Academy's per-driver training logins (T2) had nothing to attach to. **Schema (manual migration per §2.2, `20260612120000_add_driver_carrier_profile_link`):** `Driver.carrierProfileId String?` + `@@index` + `CarrierProfile.drivers Driver[] @relation("CarrierDrivers")`. Additive + nullable — pre-existing AE fleet rows keep NULL and stay invisible to carrier roster queries; `ON DELETE SET NULL` per Prisma optional-relation convention. **Backend (new [`routes/carrierDrivers.ts`](backend/src/routes/carrierDrivers.ts), ~250 LOC):** mounted at `/api/carrier-drivers` (BEFORE `/carrier` per the prefix-matching rule at index.ts:191) + added to `CARRIER_PORTAL_MOUNTS` in [middleware/auth.ts](backend/src/middleware/auth.ts) (Sprint 53.a cookie-routing lesson — new carrier mounts MUST join the allow-list or dual-portal sessions resolve the wrong cookie). `authenticate` + `authorize("CARRIER")` + `getApprovedProfile` gate (404 no-profile / 403 `CARRIER_SUSPENDED` with compliance@ message mirroring carrierLoads / 403 `CARRIER_NOT_APPROVED`). Endpoints: `GET /` roster list (lean `ROSTER_SELECT`, no pagination at launch volume, active/inactive counts); `POST /` add driver — **phone REQUIRED** (T2 issues SMS invites to it per the locked phone+PIN decision), normalized via existing `normalizePhoneE164` ([lib/phoneNormalization.ts](backend/src/lib/phoneNormalization.ts), v3.8.ala), per-carrier duplicate-phone 409 (scoped to THIS carrier — a driver may legitimately exist under another carrier's roster); `PATCH /:id` edit (ownership check returns 404 not 403 so roster ids aren't enumerable cross-carrier; phone re-normalization + self-excluding dupe check); `PATCH /:id/deactivate` → INACTIVE + `PATCH /:id/reactivate` → AVAILABLE. **NO hard delete** — training records (T3+) must survive roster churn. **Frontend:** new [`/carrier/dashboard/drivers`](frontend/src/app/carrier/dashboard/drivers/page.tsx) (~390 LOC) on the documents-page idiom (CarrierCard, gold gradient CTA, TanStack Query + api client): add/edit form (firstName/lastName/phone required + email/licenseType CDL-A-default/CDL#/state/license-expiry/med-card-expiry optional; empty optionals OMITTED from payload per EditLoadModal precedent so backend Zod optionals pass), roster table with E.164→display phone formatting, expiry-tone warnings (red past-due / amber <30d) on license + med card, Active/Inactive badges, edit/deactivate/reactivate row actions with confirm dialog, inline error banners on both form + row mutations (Item 43 silent-error lesson), empty state + post-roster Academy teaser strip (internal portal surface per §12 — not a public marketing claim; §4/§5 untouched until the Academy is live). "Drivers" sidebar entry (Users icon) between My Loads and Compliance in [CarrierSidebar.tsx](frontend/src/components/carrier/CarrierSidebar.tsx). **Audit collateral banked at Item 193:** carrier-portal nav orphans (loadboard not in sidebar; revenue/scorecard reachable only via dashboard links). **Scope:** ~700 LOC across 6 files + 1 migration. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11 (CI parity):** `prisma generate` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, backend vitest **243/243** clean, frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx next build` clean (`out/carrier/dashboard/drivers.html` confirmed in static export). **P1002 risk:** low-medium — one additive migration; Render `migrate deploy` applies via the validated post-Item-8.10 chain; retry via Manual Deploy if advisory-lock contention fires. **Patterns applied:** §3.5 audit-first (3 parallel Explore agents on portal frontend / backend / adjacent systems BEFORE design); §3.3 atomic single-sprint ship; §2.2 manual migration; §19 Sub-pattern 6 (letter verified against origin HEAD at commit time), Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity. **Patterns emerged:** none. **Letter:** amv latest origin/main HEAD; amw sequence-continuous. **Halt:** per §3.3, T2 (driver auth) waits for Wasi smoke + sign-off on deployed T1. |

| v3.8.amx | 2026-06-12 | **P0 hotfix bundle — 5 broken/prohibited items from the 2026-06-10/12 public-site enhancement audit** (19-batch read-only workflow per §20.2; 173 findings adversarially verified; Wasi GO on the P0 slice; bundle shape per v3.8.ajt/ajv precedent). **(1) BOL QR + email tracking links un-broken** — [`_redirects`](frontend/public/_redirects) `/track/*` + `/verify/*` rewrite targets flipped `.html` → extensionless (`/track`, `/verify`). Bare `/track` never matches the splat so the v3.8.ade redirect-loop class can't fire. RE-closes §13.3 Item 31's regression class — the v3.8.add `.html`-target conversion silently re-broke what v3.8.aae fixed. **(2) Mobile nav un-broken on /shippers + /track** — [`shippers.css`](frontend/public/shared/css/pages/shippers.css) styled `.nav-hamburger` (6 selectors) while chrome renders `class="hamburger"` → button invisible; renamed all 6. EIA-indexed framing rejected — not published on any deployed page (§20.8.5 disclosure-ceiling standard). **(5) /carriers hero stat corrected** — "2-Day Quick Pay Option" → "Same-Day" ([carriers.html](frontend/public/carriers.html) hero-stats); §8 LOCKED offers 7-day + same-day only, and /faq itself rebuts 2-day as competitor framing. **Scope:** ~90 LOC net across 8 files, zero backend, zero schema. **Pre-commit gates per Sub-pattern 11:** backend tsc + vitest clean (defensive — untouched), frontend tsc + `npx next build` clean. **Post-deploy gates:** curl `/track/<token>` → 200 URL-preserved; grep deployed `/` for zero "Tier-graduated"; grep deployed `/carriers` for zero "2-Day"; deployed shippers.css contains `.hamburger`. **Remaining audit output (168 P1-P3 findings) awaits Wasi ratification as Sprints 2-5** (injector/head hygiene → honesty alignment → a11y/forms → polish). **Patterns applied:** §3.5 audit-first (every fix site read + root-caused before edit); §3.2 rendered-output verification (pre-fix repro + post-deploy gates on deployed URLs); §19 sub-rule c (extensionless-target pattern verified live on /quote/approve before adoption; EIA claim checked against deployed /carriers before tile text chosen); §19 Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity. **Patterns emerged:** none — the _redirects regression is a recurrence of the deferred-risk class; the `.html`-target normalization behavior is now documented inline in _redirects so the class can't silently recur. **Letter:** amw latest HEAD; amx sequence-continuous. |

| v3.8.amy | 2026-06-12 | **Audit Sprint 2 — injector + head hygiene (Wasi GO).** The chrome injector was BOTH the regression source and the fix point. **Root-cause discovery:** `inject-chrome.mjs` runs as the `prebuild` npm hook, so it fires on every Cloudflare `npm run build` but NOT on a bare local `npx next build` — which is why the v3.8.ams penguin-gold fix and the /shippers og:image hand-edit kept silently reverting on deploy while local gates stayed green. Going-forward gate: frontend build verification for any sprint touching `frontend/public/*.html` or the injector MUST use `npm run build` (exercises prebuild + proves injector idempotency), not bare `npx next build`. **Injector fixes ([inject-chrome.mjs](frontend/scripts/inject-chrome.mjs)):** (1) `PENGUIN_SVG` gold `#C8963E` → `#BA7517` ×3 — the constant was the source of truth overwriting the page-level fix every build. (2) `pathToCanonical` emits extensionless URLs (`/carriers` not `/carriers.html`) — kills the canonical-to-308-redirect chain on all 11 non-home pages + og:url mirror. (3) `renderMeta` gains per-page social image via marker attribute (`<!-- INCLUDE:meta og-image="..." -->`); `replaceMeta` now PRESERVES marker attributes on rewrite (previously emitted a bare marker, silently dropping them); `parseAttrs` regex `\w+` → `[\w-]+` (hyphenated attr names — caught by post-run verification when `og-image` parsed as `image`; sub-rule c fire). /shippers marker carries its hero (`/media/shipper-hero.jpg`). (4) Chrome now ships the Sign-In dropdown wiring: new [`shared/js/nav-login.js`](frontend/public/shared/js/nav-login.js) (click toggle + aria-expanded sync + outside-click + Escape close + double-bind guard via `data-srl-login-wired`) emitted inside INCLUDE:nav on all 13 pages. Pre-amy the dropdown was keyboard/touch-dead on 11 of 12 pages — only index.html had working wiring (retired in favor of the shared file); six pages (about/blog/careers/carriers/faq/shippers) shipped dead `#loginDropdown` blocks targeting elements that don't exist (all deleted, tombstone comments left). [`utilities.css`](frontend/public/shared/css/utilities.css) adds `:focus-within` to the dropdown-open selector group as a no-JS keyboard fallback. Injector design-note comment rewritten (chrome ships login wiring; hamburger stays page-local — a chrome-shipped duplicate would double-toggle). **Head hygiene (10 pages):** legacy duplicate OG/Twitter blocks deleted (index/contact/faq/blog/careers/privacy/terms) — retired tagline "Your Freight. Our Legacy.", `www.` og:urls, and `MC 01794414` are gone from the machine-readable layer; **MC verified against FMCSA SAFER live (`MC-1794414` / SILK ROUTE LOGISTICS INC) before correcting per §3.13.** Stray `/logo.svg` + `/logo.png` + duplicate `favicon.ico` icon links and duplicate preconnect pairs removed; `keywords` metas dropped site-wide; author/robots/titles kept. **Meta copy:** /blog descriptions no longer claim "curated daily by AI" (×4 — aligned to the v3.8.amf hero canonical "from across the industry, updated throughout the day"); index/contact/blog/shippers descriptions de-em-dashed; index description MC corrected; /faq retitled "Freight Brokerage FAQ \| Silk Route Logistics" (was FAQ-doubled, zero category keyword); /shippers title 77→59 chars. **sitemap.xml:** extensionless locs, `lastmod` added, `/verify` dropped (noindex token utility). **Scope:** ~200 LOC net across 17 files (injector + nav-login.js + utilities.css + 12 page heads + sitemap + VersionFooter + CLAUDE.md); injector re-run regenerated all 13 marker blocks; second run rewrote 0 (idempotency proven). **Gates:** frontend tsc clean + `npm run build` clean (prebuild injector rewrote 0, 109 static pages); backend tsc + vitest clean (defensive). **Post-deploy gates:** deployed canonicals extensionless, penguin `#BA7517` live, zero `01794414` / retired tagline / "curated daily by AI" on deployed heads, nav-login.js 200, dropdown opens via keyboard. **Patterns applied:** §3.5 audit-first; §3.13 (SAFER primary-source check); §3.2 rendered-output verification; §19 sub-rule c (parseAttrs hyphen bug caught by post-run grep verification before commit); Sub-pattern 11 CI-parity — extended: the npm-script-hook divergence (`npm run build` vs `npx next build`) is a NEW case of local-gate-vs-deploy-gate mismatch, banked above. **Remaining audit roadmap:** Sprint 3 honesty alignment, Sprint 4 a11y + forms, Sprint 5 polish — awaiting GO. **Letter:** amx latest HEAD; amy sequence-continuous. |

| v3.8.axe | 2026-09-01 | **Tender lifecycle Phase B, commit 6b of 12 — create a tender in a transaction and its history vanished.** The last three creators move onto `createTender` (waterfall auto-pilot, load+tender drawer, load-board bid accept): **6 → 0**. The auto-pilot needed its `$transaction` converted from the ARRAY form to the INTERACTIVE one, because `createTender` is async (it must write a transition row) and an async call cannot be an element of `$transaction([...])` — awaiting it to build the array would run the insert outside the transaction and lose atomicity with the status flip. **That conversion exposed a bug introduced in the same arc:** `createTender` writes the tender AND an activity row carrying an FK to it, and the `db` argument reached the insert but not the transition write — so inside a caller transaction the history row referenced a tender nothing outside could see, and Postgres rejected it. **The consequence is worse than an error:** `logTenderTransition` is deliberately non-throwing, so it SWALLOWED the rejection and the tender committed with **no history at all** — nothing failed, nothing logged at error level, the drawer just showed an empty timeline. TypeScript cannot see it (both calls compile); a mocked Prisma cannot either (no FKs). **Verified** by [`_create-tender-proof.ts`](backend/scripts/_create-tender-proof.ts) **17/17** including rollback of both rows; adversarially verified — reverting the one `db` argument gives **16/17**, failing exactly the transition-committed assertion with zero rows. **An existing invariant test went red — a mock gap, not a logic break** (`waterfallTenderInvariant` did not define `logTenderTransition`; §19 Sub-pattern 11 case study 3), added to the mock **without aliasing**, since aliasing hides that exact divergence. **Three assertions re-aimed from the array SHAPE to the intent** — re-aimed, not relaxed: removing the flip from the transaction still turns two red, verified by injection. **Threshold note:** five source files, one over, because splitting the `db`-threading fix from the two callers needing it would leave a committed intermediate with the silent-history-loss bug live — the threshold serves reviewability, and a knowingly broken intermediate commit serves nothing. **Outbound (§19 SP20):** keys explicitly empty, `[Email] Sent to` count **0**, measured. **Gates:** backend tsc clean, vitest **134/1**, frontend tsc + build clean. |

| v3.8.axh | 2026-09-01 | **Tender lifecycle Phase B, commit 7 of 12 — a countered tender had no way to be turned down.** A carrier could counter and an AE could *accept* it (accept-on-behalf admits COUNTERED and settles at the counter rate via `agreedRateFromTender`), but **rejecting one had no path at all** — the tender sat at COUNTERED until it expired, which reads to the carrier as being ignored. New `POST /tenders/:id/reject-counter` (AE-only; SRL's decision, not the carrier's) moves it to **WITHDRAWN, never DECLINED**: the carrier made an offer and SRL turned it down, and DECLINED feeds `tendersDeclined`/`acceptanceRate` which §9 scores at 10% of Compass — recording SRL's refusal there would mark a carrier **for having negotiated**. It deliberately does **not** silently re-offer the original rate: a carrier who countered has said that number does not work, so reviving it re-offers something already refused; re-tendering is a fresh `createTender`. **`LoadTender.version`** arrives with it (gap row 8, additive, default 1) — a counter changes the TERMS, and without a revision "which rate did the carrier sign for" was answerable only by comparing timestamps. **A counter VOIDs a live RC** (an RC names a rate; once countered a live one states a number neither side agrees to, on the document a dispute turns on) while **SIGNED and FINALIZED are excluded** — executed evidence is not something a counter-offer gets to rewrite. **Verified** by [`_counter-lifecycle-proof.ts`](backend/scripts/_counter-lifecycle-proof.ts) **13/13** against a real database; adversarially verified — removing the executed-RC exclusion gives 12/13. **Outbound (§19 SP20):** keys explicitly empty, `[Email] Sent to` count **0**, measured. **Gates:** backend tsc clean, vitest **135/1**, frontend tsc + build clean. |

| v3.8.axi | 2026-09-01 | **Tender lifecycle Phase B, commit 8a of 12 — a load could be back on the board with a tender still claiming it.** `fallOffRecovery` cleared the carrier and re-posted the load but left the tender reading `ACCEPTED` **forever** — nothing in the data said the carrier had gone — and it was the *only* path that took a carrier off a load at all. New [`carrierReleaseService.ts`](backend/src/services/carrierReleaseService.ts): `releaseCarrier` does the whole act in one transaction (carrier off, tender → `RELEASED` with a coded reason, live RCs voided, load returned to its origin path, history written, fall-off recorded), plus new `TenderStatus.RELEASED` in its own migration because Postgres refuses to *use* a new enum value in the transaction that added it. **Reasons:** `carrier_fell_off`, `compliance_lapse`, `rate_dispute`, `customer_cancel`, `srl_error`. **`srl_error` records NO fall-off** — fall-off count feeds carrier standing, so marking a carrier for a load SRL took away by mistake is charging them for our error; `customer_cancel` and `compliance_lapse` DO count (the load still fell off; the second is the carrier's own paperwork), and whether those should *weigh* the same as walking away is a scoring question, not this service's job. **Release is not withdraw, deliberately separate:** withdraw pulls an offer nobody accepted (cheap, reversible, no reason), release takes back a committed load with a truck possibly routed and paper signed — so `withdrawTender` **refuses an ACCEPTED tender** and points the caller at release. **Origin-aware return:** a waterfall load goes back to its cascade, a board load to the board; sending a waterfall load to POSTED would strand it, since the cascade drives it and nothing re-enters a cascade from POSTED. Executed (`SIGNED`/`FINALIZED`) RCs are untouched. **Idempotent** — releasing a carrier-less load returns `released: false` rather than throwing, and records no second fall-off. **Verified** by [`_release-carrier-proof.ts`](backend/scripts/_release-carrier-proof.ts) **24/24**; adversarially verified — removing `srl_error` from the no-fault set gives 22/24. **The guard caught the new writer** and was updated to 8 entries; the bulk-withdraw consolidation that shrinks it is 8b. **Outbound (§19 SP20):** keys explicitly empty, count **0**, measured. **Gates:** backend tsc clean, vitest **135/1**, frontend tsc + build clean. |
| v3.8.axj | 2026-09-01 | **Tender lifecycle Phase B, commit 8b of 12 — six places withdrew sibling tenders, and all six missed the same one.** Accept, accept-on-behalf, broadcast accept, load cancelled, cascade position skipped and compliance block each ran their own sibling `updateMany`, and every one filtered on `status: "OFFERED"` alone — so a **COUNTERED** sibling survived being covered by another carrier, stayed live, and could afterwards be accepted onto a load that was already booked. New [`tenderTransitionService.ts`](backend/src/services/tenderTransitionService.ts): `withdrawLiveTenders` withdraws every live tender in a scope (a load, or a cascade position), optionally sparing the winner, with a coded reason from a fixed vocabulary and one transition row per tender; `LIVE` is `OFFERED | COUNTERED`, which is what all six sites meant. Migrated here: both accept paths in `tenderController`, `broadcastTenderService`, `integrationService` (8c takes the rest). **The gap did not need finding — it needed the six copies to become one place where the question is asked once.** A vocabulary six files each maintain separately is not a vocabulary, which is how `EXPIRED` came to mean "SRL pulled the offer" in two of them and `DECLINED` in two others, every one a mark on a carrier's acceptance rate (§9, 10% of Compass) for doing nothing. **`respondedAt` is never set on a withdraw** — nobody responded, and stamping a response time would make a carrier look like they answered. **`softDelete` is opt-in**: only the cancelled-load path wants it, because hiding a tender from a carrier's own history is right when the load is gone and wrong every other time. **The accept paths move from an array `$transaction` to an interactive one** — the helper reads before it writes (a history row needs each sibling's id and FROM state; `updateMany` returns neither) and issues one transition per sibling, which composable promises cannot express. **The race is handled rather than assumed away:** the update is scoped to the live states so a just-accepted sibling is never clobbered, and when the counts disagree the rows that actually moved are re-read before anything is logged. **Guard:** `loadTenderWriters` now asserts the **length** of the state-writer list, not only membership — membership alone is satisfied by *adding* a file, which is the drift the list exists to prevent; seven today (was eight), target three (create / transition / release). Its stale-entry check earned its place immediately, failing on the two files the moment they stopped writing status. **Proof:** [`_withdraw-consolidation-proof.ts`](backend/scripts/_withdraw-consolidation-proof.ts) **26/26** against a real database, calling the real helper rather than a copy of the write (§13.3 Item 222.5). **Adversarial:** reverting `LIVE` to OFFERED-only → **18/26**, naming the countered sibling first. **Recorded honestly:** dropping the `db` argument leaves the commit assertion GREEN and turns only the rollback one red — unlike `createTender` the tender already exists, so the FK holds either way and only a rollback separates them; the proof now says which case catches it, because a green assertion pointing at the wrong mechanism is the §19 Sub-pattern 16 shape. **Outbound (§19 SP20):** keys explicitly empty, count **0**, measured. **Gates:** backend tsc clean, vitest **1418/1** (known `urlSafety` DNS red), frontend tsc + build clean. |

The check moved to `settleTender`, where the actor is known: `DECLINED` is refused unless the actor is the carrier or the caller passes `onBehalf`, and an AE recording a phoned-in decline is kept distinct in the transition row rather than presented as the carrier's own click. **The expiry sweep gained history it never had** — `processExpiredTenders` flipped rows with `updateMany` and wrote nothing, so a tender that ran out of time left no trace. **Two scanner blind spots, both found by the guards themselves:** the helper takes `data` as a parameter, so a literal-key scanner reported the transition service as writing no status at all (its own stale-entry check caught it) — an unreadable payload now counts as a status write, because the alternative is a guard with a documented bypass; and the `DECLINED` allow-list is now **empty** rather than one entry, since the service writes `status: input.to` and the literal appears nowhere. **A defect the type system could not see:** `settleTender` first defaulted its FROM rail to every non-terminal state by name including `RC_SENT`/`CONFIRMED` — ratified but not in the Prisma enum until commit 11 — so every rail-less call died on *"Invalid value for argument `in`"*; the list is cast through `as never` to reach the generated enum type, and a cast is a promise that the check is unnecessary. Both answers refuse and nothing unsafe happened, but a block that names the wrong remedy sends somebody to re-type a code that was correct. New `LoadTender.complianceOverrideId` (additive, nullable, migration `20260901060000_load_tender_compliance_override`), written by `tenderCreationService` and mirrored into the opening transition's metadata; `complianceCheck` now returns `appliedOverrideId` so callers do not re-derive it. **A FK, not three copied columns** — `ComplianceOverride` already holds who (`adminId`), why (`reason`) and when (`createdAt`), and copying them would create a second answer to "who waived this" that can drift from the first. **Recorded only when an override actually RELEASED something:** a live override that waived nothing did not put this carrier on this load, and stamping it would make an audit read a waiver into an ordinary tender — both directions asserted, the clean tender must come back null. **No backfill** (a historical tender genuinely has no recorded override; inventing one is worse than the gap) and `ON DELETE SET NULL` so removing an override row cannot take the tender with it. **Proof:** [`_hard-fail-refusal-proof.ts`](backend/scripts/_hard-fail-refusal-proof.ts) extended to **23/23**; adversarial — dropping the single argument that carries it gives **21/23**. **One of my own assertions was vacuous:** the history check compared the metadata field against the id, and when the id was null it matched a metadata field that was also null, **passing while proving nothing**; it now requires a non-null id first. | v3.8.bcu | 2026-09-18 | **A dropped RSC fetch no longer lands a carrier on the raw payload — §13.3 Items 156 + 157 CLOSED, with the mechanism they guessed at corrected.** Reported from a carrier's iPhone: tapping through from the tender email showed `/carrier/login` as a page of `1:"$Sreact.fragment" 2:I[26264,…` — the React Server Components flight payload rendered as the document. **Three hypotheses eliminated before the fix, each by evidence rather than reading:** direct GETs served HTML under every request shape tried (iOS Safari UA, `Accept: */*`, `text/plain`, `?next=`), so the Item 156 "Gmail Accept-header negotiation" story does not reproduce; the router's build-ID-mismatch fallback was proven to strip `.txt` by injecting a stale build ID into the flight response in a real browser (hard-navigated to `/carrier/forgot-password`, HTML); and both files carry `no-cache, must-revalidate`, so Safari's HTTP cache cannot hand back the wrong body. **The defect is in Next 15.5.14's `fetch-server-response.js`:** in `output:"export"` mode `url` is mutated in place to the flight URL (`/carrier/login` → `/carrier/login.txt?_rsc=…`) before fetching, and the `catch` block hand-builds its fallback as `flightData: url.toString()` — the mutated URL — where every sibling fallback routes through `doMpaNavigation()`, which strips `.txt` and `_rsc`. A string `flightData` is a hard navigation, so **any thrown fetch** sends the browser to the `.txt` as a document. On a phone that is an ordinary dropped connection (`TypeError: Load failed`), or Safari aborting the in-flight fetch through the module's own `pagehide` listener when a competing hard navigation commits — which is exactly what the unauthenticated `/carrier/dashboard/*` path does (see Item 275). **Reproduced against production:** rejecting the `.txt` fetch, one link tap navigated the browser to `/carrier/forgot-password.txt` and rendered the payload. Upstream fixed it in canary (`return originalUrl.toString()`); 15.5.x carries it. **Fix:** `frontend/patches/next+15.5.14.patch` makes the catch use `doMpaNavigation` in both dist copies (CJS + ESM — the browser bundle is built from ESM per Next's own compiler aliases); `postinstall` applies it and fails the install if it no longer fits; a new `prebuild` gate (`scripts/check-next-patch.mjs`) refuses to build if the ESM copy is unpatched, so an install that skipped scripts cannot ship the bug silently. **Guard** `src/lib/nextRscFallback.test.ts` calls the real function with a rejecting fetch — it was **red on the unpatched module for exactly `…/carrier/login.txt?next=…`** before the patch — with a vacuity tripwire proving the export branch ran; it mirrors Next's own `react-server-dom-webpack/client` → `compiled/…/client.browser` alias at the resolver so it runs against the shipped dependency, not a stub. **Adversarial:** the gate refuses an unpatched tree; `patch-package` re-applies from the unpatched shape byte-identically. **My own test was wrong once:** its structural window was 900 bytes and the patched return sits at 909 — red against a correct file; now bounded by the next function definition. **Gates:** frontend tsc clean, vitest **184/184**, `npm run build` clean through the new prebuild gate. Backend untouched and its suite deliberately not run: the working tree holds another session's uncommitted compliance work, and a result there would describe their tree, not this commit. |

Nothing existed — no tour library, no first-run hook, no "seen" flag. **Trigger:** the carrier layout already computes `showOperationalChrome = APPROVED && activated (BCA signed) && authenticator enrolled` for the chrome; the tour mounts on that same condition plus an **explicit** `portalTourCompletedAt === null` from `/activation-status`. See §13.3 Item 306. **Deliberate live consequence:** the backfill takes customer operational mail to **zero recipients** until an AE ticks the new box — ratified, not a defect. 13 contacts exist, 8 were eligible under the old rule, 0 after. | v3.8.bjb | 2026-09-24 | **Cloudflare Pages deploy verification reads the bytes the site serves, replacing an Actions job name that never could.** Pages builds outside GitHub Actions entirely, so no Actions job — green, red, or absent — was ever evidence about what Cloudflare served; citing one was the §19 Sub-pattern 16 shape. **The commit was already being computed and discarded:** `next.config.ts:13` has read `CF_PAGES_COMMIT_SHA` into `NEXT_PUBLIC_BUILD_ID` for a long time and `config/env.ts:45` exports it as `BUILD_ID` with ZERO importers. Three parts, each closing a way the check could lie: `stamp-build.mjs` runs as `postbuild` (so it fires on Cloudflare's own `npm run build`) and writes `out/build-info.json` recording the sha AND its `source`, so a Cloudflare build is distinguishable from a laptop build; a `_headers` rule marks that path `no-store`, without which the edge could answer from cache and the check would pass on the PREVIOUS deploy; and `check-pages-deploy.mjs` polls the live origin comparing SERVED to pushed, with a distinct exit code per failure because the remedies differ (1 late/failed/never-triggered · 2 no marker at all · 3 something else answering · 4 could not run) and **never exit 0 on "could not tell"**. It deliberately does not read the Cloudflare API: the API reports what Cloudflare RECORDED, this reports what a browser RECEIVES, and only the second is what a halt card claims — and no credential for it exists in the repo, the environment, or GitHub secrets, so that branch would have shipped unexercisable (Item 238). **A defect only the real artifact surfaced:** all ten exit paths were correct against a localhost fixture, and the first run against production returned the right verdict then exited **127** — `process.exit()` inside a fetch continuation crashes Node on Windows, and 127 conventionally means "command not found", so a caller would have read it as the script being missing rather than as the deploy verdict. Localhost HTTP tears its socket down cleanly; only real TLS tripped it. Restructured to set `process.exitCode`. **Verified on the real platform:** Cloudflare serves `source: "CF_PAGES_COMMIT_SHA"`, `ref: "main"`, with the no-store header live and no `cf-cache-status` — which also answers by observation the one thing that could not be read from here, namely that Cloudflare runs `npm run build` and therefore fires the postbuild hook. Guard `pagesDeployCheck.test.ts` (7 cases) holds the two halves wired and agreeing; six injections each failed exactly its own assertion. One of them found a real weakness in the guard itself — it asserted `toContain("CF_PAGES_COMMIT_SHA")` and stayed green when the stamp was repointed at a different variable, because the string also appears as the `source:` label, so it would have passed a marker that LABELS its sha as Cloudflare's while reading something else. Recorded in CLAUDE.md §2.5 beside the halt-card format, which that section is now stated to define. |
| v3.8.bho → v3.8.bht | 2026-09-23 | **The bill of lading stops naming the billing contact as the dock contact, and starts saying which template drew it.** Six commits. `bho` the BOL drops `load.customer?.contactName` — a load with no stop contact printed whoever the CRM holds for BILLING, and on SRL-121497 that was the customer's AP contact, on the document a driver hands over at a dock; new `lib/stopContact.ts` is the single answer to "who is at this dock", tiered load contact → linked facility → unique normalized name+city match, and **the billing contact is never a tier**. `bhp` the rate confirmation shares that resolver and drops its own billing fallback, gaining contact email. `bhq` the `[HH:MM–HH:MM]` placeholder that printed on 4 of 7 live loads is gone; `lib/stopWindow.ts` renders what is actually recorded, with the suffix "local" because **no timezone is stored anywhere and both states SRL ships are split-zone, so an abbreviation could only be a guess**; two dead `appointmentRequired` reads removed (the Load field they cited has never existed — see §13.3 Item 307). `bhr` template v2.10: an empty field renders BLANK rather than a bracketed italic label or an em-dash, because on a dock the honest rendering of "we do not have this" is space somebody can write in — Special Instructions keeps "None" deliberately, being the one field where blank space invites an instruction the carrier never agreed to. **The Released Value "collision" was a misreading**: measured, the italic run ended at x448.4 against an initial block starting at x450, so 1.6pt of deterministic clearance, never an overprint — re-spaced to 7pt anyway because 1.6pt at 7.75pt type reads as touching. `bhs` Section 7 non-recourse leaves the consignee column and is **not** relocated; six placements measured, none viable, banked at §13.3 Item 308. `bht` both documents now name their template version from one constant, distinct from `RC_TERMS_VERSION` — the slot is measured, since the existing terms-version line clips off a 792pt page for the BOL's overridden footer. **Twelve of fifteen render pins are byte-identical**, which is the proof the chrome widening is inert where the option is omitted. Gates: anchors +0 throughout; verify:bol and verify:rc all cases pass, one page. |
| v3.8.bji → v3.8.bjj | 2026-09-25 | **The audit row names the entity again, and the status-machine gate counts every writer.** Write-path + record-repair arc, branch `arc/writepath-repair` off `924b5431`. `bji` — `auditMiddleware` writes from `res.on("finish")`, by which point Express has rewritten `req.url` to the innermost mount-relative path, so `parseEntityFromPath` was handed `/<id>/status` and wrote the LOAD ID into `entityType` (uppercased) with the action word into `entityId`. Now reads `req.originalUrl`, which Express sets once and never rewrites — immune to mounting BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by being read early enough. **The arc's own premise undercounted the population by two orders of magnitude**: briefed as ~47 rows, measured read-only as `srl_readonly` at **3,408 of 3,799 (90%)**, both date ranges ending the same day. `entityId = 'status'` was 63 of those — one subset; the dominant shape is `entityId = 'unknown'` (3,355), which is what a single-segment mount-relative path yields. Historical rows left alone: they faithfully record WHICH REQUEST happened under two columns that name it wrongly, and re-deriving `entityType` from a path recorded by the same defect would be inventing history from the artifact of the bug. `bjj` — the enforcement gate (§13.3 Item 194) was fed by a `$allOperations` client extension, which sees only the SHARED Prisma client. SRL-121496 was reversed twice and counted once, purely by which client wrote it. An `AFTER UPDATE OF "status"` trigger on `loads` now writes every transition to `load_status_transitions`, and BOTH health pairs derive from that one log — the since-boot pair moved too, because leaving it client-side would have given `/api/health` two derivations that disagree the first time a foreign client moves a status. **The counter table is FROZEN, not dropped**: `cumulative_since` is the soak's start date, so reading only the log would have restarted Item 194's clock. Two disjoint windows, not two answers. The log carries `loadId`, so the strict per-event `wasAuthorisedUncancel` replaces the conservative window subtraction for the log era. The observer keeps its log line, stops counting, and its header now says it is explicitly NOT the source of truth. **Proof `_arc-c1-foreign-client-proof.ts` 15/15**, whose control drops the trigger and runs the identical script, client and edge — nothing logged, gate unmoved: the pre-C1 world reproduced rather than described. Injection forcing `logEdges` empty gives 12/15, failing exactly the gate-delta assertions while the log-reading ones stay green. **Guard** `ownPrismaClientCensus.test.ts` freezes the 39-of-151 tracked scripts holding their own client; only the MAINTENANCE bucket ratchets, because a ceiling raised by every arc that writes a proof teaches the reflex of raising it. Gates: backend tsc clean, vitest 298 files / 3139 passed / 5 skipped / 0 failed. |

There was no exit from SUSPENDED: `approveCarrier` refused a suspended carrier ("must have suspension lifted before approval"), `rejectCarrier` refused one ("lift suspension first"), and nothing provided the action both named. Found when the owner needed BLUE FALCON BROKERAGE LLC's login for portal testing. `bke`: `POST /compliance/carrier/:carrierId/lift-suspension` beside the suspend route, same roles (ADMIN / CEO / OPERATIONS), reason required. **Lands on REVIEWING, never APPROVED** (restore's B6c reasoning: approving is a separate decision). Clears the three auto-suspend columns in the same conditional write (`updateMany WHERE SUSPENDED AND not archived`, 409 `SUSPENSION_CHANGED` if the status moved underneath). They are not inert: `loadComplianceService` flags any load on a carrier with `autoSuspendedAt` CRITICAL, and waterfall scoring excludes it. ### §11 — Architectural finding (cross-cutting, surfaced during v3.8.d sprint, 2026-04-28)

`sanitizeInput` middleware at `backend/src/middleware/security.ts` (registered at `server.ts:150`) was HTML-escaping all `req.body` string values at write time, causing values to be stored encoded in the database (e.g., `equipmentType: 'Dry Van 53&#x27;'` instead of `'Dry Van 53''`). Every human-facing read path required compensating decode logic at the output boundary. Worse, repeated re-saves through the same middleware would multi-encode (`&amp;#x27;`, `&amp;amp;#x27;`, etc.) which one-pass decoders couldn't fully reverse.

**Tactical fix at v3.8.d:** scoped `decodeHtmlEntities` at `/tracking` public serializer; PDFKit's `safe()` already handled PDF rendering.

**Architectural fix at v3.8.d.2:** middleware rewritten to do proper INPUT hygiene only — trim, length-cap, null-byte strip — without OUTPUT encoding. Output-layer escaping moved to where it belongs:
- HTML rendering → React's automatic JSX escaping
- PDF rendering → PDFKit's `safe()` decode at the boundary
- JSON APIs → JSON-encoding handles its own char-set; no HTML escaping needed

**Pattern for new surfaces:** any new customer-facing surface that reads from the database must NOT assume strings are clean if any pre-v3.8.d.2 data may be present. Use `decodeHtmlEntities` defensively until a full data audit confirms clean. v3.8.d.2 included a one-time decode script at `backend/scripts/decode-encoded-load-fields.ts` (idempotent, multi-pass-safe per v3.8.d.3 update) for cleanup of legacy encoded data.

**Lesson for future architectural decisions:** input-time HTML escaping is double-escape on output anyway (templating engines auto-escape). Defense-in-depth is good; doing the same defense twice in different layers is corruption.
